## §9 THE CLANDESTINE HIRE — A COURT'S SECRET, AND ITS SCANDAL (CH-7)

**The owner (message 6).** "courts can hire villains. but such hiring is clandestine. there should be a system of confirmation based on our spy system that if let out, would damage thecourt's repuation."

**The hire.** A retainer with `covert: true` (the `covertHire`) — taken by a court for a VILLAIN only, and EVERY engine-made villain retainer is one (CH21), through the same offer hearing (the card says "in secret"). The hire is the employing court's secret; the villain's `secretKind` is `hidden_employer`.

**ONE IDIOM, THREE TIES.** The secret, the leak, the belief ladder and the scandal below are ONE idiom for every clandestine tie a court holds: (a) a VILLAIN PARAGON's covert retainer (this volume); (b) a court's `employed` GANG — THE BODIES' court↔gang stance, always covert (BD §7.1; "a TIE, never a hire into the manpower pool"); (c) a foreign court's SPONSORSHIP of a gang or a conclave (THE BODIES' item 46: "a CLANDESTINE TIE … between a SPONSOR … and a CLIENT body"; beasts never). Each tie's SECRET is its own record's (the paragon's `retainer`; BD's stance row and sponsorship tie); its LEAKS are tellings of RN's `TELLING_SOURCES` — CH's paragon row below, and THE BODIES' body row (`leak:body:<bodyId>:<courtId>:<sinceTick>`, `courtId` the EMPLOYING or the SPONSORING court — an employed gang's hire or a sponsored body's tie, ONE PER TIE; RN §3.4 registers the row) (BD writes the slot as `<employerId>`, BD §7.1, and as `<sponsorId>`, BD §10.4) whose content declares BD's rumour slots (`bodyId`, `bodyKind`, BD §2.4) in place of `paragonId` and the same `what: 'paragon_leak'` — and a CAUGHT carrier (L-BD 47: a courier caught on a way, a smuggler's silver seized, a cache found at a junction a warden knows) is road 3's caught run; the BELIEF of who holds the hidden hand is ONE store with ONE writer for all three, P3's `paragonBeliefs[observerId][subjectId]` written by CH's pass in THE ONE PERSON STAGE from each road's landed record (the arrival, ES's product, the town's-gate or patrol receipt); the EXPOSURE is ONE call of `applyForeignExposureBlowback` with the ONE source `covert_hire_exposed`, made by CH's leaf `paragon/covertHire.js :: exposeCovertHire(worldState, { tieKind, subjectId, employerId, workedAgainstId, importance, name, tick })` — `tieKind ∈ retainer · employed · sponsorship` (CH's word for a paragon's covert retainer and THE BODIES' two, each in its owner's meaning) carried on the exposure's RECEIPT and on its one `covert_hire_exposed` DEED (minted by the leaf through RN's writer: doer the employer court, subject the hidden hand — `p:<wnpcId>` or the body's RN key — §2.2b), NEVER into the landed blowback source, whose reader copies only `settlementId · patronId · patronKind · name · importance · kind` (`corruptionWeb.js :: foreignExposuresFrom`, CONFIRMED at `81f1cd963`) — a SHARED LEAF OWNED BY CH (the shared-leaf rule: "each shared leaf is cut as its OWN unit … a caller never re-implements") — CH's pass calls it for a paragon's retainer, THE BODIES' stage for a body's tie — and ONE LATCH PER TIE, on the tie's own record, written by the tie's owner in the act that fires it (the paragon's `retainer.exposedAt`; BD's `exposedAt` on its stance row and its sponsorship tie, BD's to write), so no tie is exposed twice and no stage writes another's ledger. What a body's exposure ADDS is THE BODIES' (item 46's three moves: the host's responsibility re-aimed at the sponsor, the `sponsor_exposed` hearing, the stance turned to `hunted`; item 46b's escalation ladder), each child receipt `causedBy` the exposure.

**How the secret leaks — four roads, each belief-decided.**
1. **Rumour.** Each week the hire stands and the villain's exertion against a court is `exerted`, a keyed hash (`hash01('paragon.leak.<wnpcId>.<tick>')`, DRAFT base rate scaled by the villain's fame and the employer's distance) decides whether a telling "<the villain> works for <employer>" is SEEDED — and it is seeded INSIDE the rumour stage, because a secret is never a public feed entry and the person stage (after the rumours) has no seeding seam: CH REGISTERS the `leak` row of RN's `TELLING_SOURCES` (RN §2.4, §3.4: "a closed registration list of NON-DEED tellings RN's one telling call seeds", read inside `renown/deedTelling.js`, the ONE call in `rumorNetwork.js :: advanceRumorLedgers`), so CH adds no host line; RN records that the ≤ 3-line fallback in `rumorNetwork.js` "is not needed", PLAUSIBLE until CH-7's car-0 measures the row's inputs. THE ROW is a pure function of `(worldState, tick, { digest, season })` — RN's widened signature (RN §3.4: "the stage's own digest and the season the kernel hands the rumour stage") — returning RN's seed shape `{ eventRef, eventTick, originIds, significance, score, content, relayedTick?, arrivalTick? }` (the leak declares neither stamp and relays as a feed seed; the findings row declares both, §8): for each paragon whose `retainer.covert` stands and whose `exertion` reads `{ tick: tick − 1, outcome: 'exerted' }`, the keyed hash decides, the worked-against town being `exertion.settlementId` — where the exertion served, never where the record stands this tick (a dark-arm `assign` may have moved it since). Its inputs were written by the person stage LAST tick, because the rumour stage runs before the person stage in the kernel's order — so a leak lags its exertion by one week, §6.3's t+1. The telling's origin is the worked-against town (`originIds`; the slander idiom, so the source is traceable) and its `eventRef` is `leak:<wnpcId>:<employerId>:<retainer.sinceTick>` — one per HIRE (a key per villain–employer pair would let a second hire of the same villain by the same court reuse the first hire's record; vetoable). ITS DECLARED SIGNIFICANCE AND SCORE: `significance: 'notable'` (its TTL 13 weeks) and `score: 0`, so the tale travels at most two hops from the worked-against town (`maxHopsFor` gives a `notable` record 2 — EXECUTED, `cure/D-CH-r3-probe/findingsHome.out`). ITS DECLARED CONTENT (RN copies only the fields a row declares — `captureContent`'s law, "ONLY structured fields are copied" — and each declared field's lifecycle row is CH's, §21 L9): `what: 'paragon_leak'`; `whereId: ''` and `partyIds: []`, so the secret refreshes no settlement's belief slot (`beliefMap.js :: reportsBySubject` reads a record as a report about S only for S in `whereId` ∪ `partyIds`); `scope: 'settlement'`; `magnitude: 0`; `causeClass` and `deityName` null; `visibility: 'mechanical'`, the landed state-only marker, so no rumour panel lists the secret (`settlementRumors.js` keeps only records whose `content?.visibility !== 'mechanical'`) and the accusation's one surface is CH's own dossier line in the court's voice (below); and two structured fields, `paragonId` (the villain's `wnpc_`) and `employerId` (the court). A secret about a person and a court moves no town's picture and appears on no rumour panel (vetoable: the alternative names the worked-against town and lets its belief slot refresh, a disclosed shift either way — RN §3.4). The ONE PERSON STAGE then reads this tick's ARRIVALS of such tellings and writes the hearing court's `paragonBeliefs` row at `vague`; a later FRESH arrival raises it one step (`vague → uncertain`, the rumour road's ceiling) (nothing written inside the rumour stage survives but `rumors.next`). A fresh arrival is rare by construction, and the volume says so: a second leak of one hire inside the TTL FOLDS into the record the court already holds (`rumorNetwork.js :: mergeArrival` keeps the first-heard `arrivalTick` — "FIRST-HEARD wins the arrival"; EXECUTED: a re-seed at tick 14 merged with `arrivalTick` 10), so a court's band rises again only after its copy has expired (the `notable` TTL: more than 13 weeks after it arrived — EXECUTED, `findingsHome.out`: a record that arrived at tick 10 is gone at tick 24) or with a NEW hire's `eventRef`.
2. **An enemy spy's `confirm`.** ES's product stage gains ONE confirm/refute SUBJECT KIND, `paragon_employer`, whose product writes the observer's `paragonBeliefs` row (a confirm raises it to `confident`, a refute lowers it) — never a `LEG_SLOTS` member (those are the ACQUIRE appraisal legs keyed to settlement belief slots; "A confirm crosses no observed slot") — SLOT C1 (espionage lit nowhere); master's products carry no subject-kind concept (`espionageProducts.js`: confirm/refute cross a SETTLEMENT's belief slots), so the kind is ES's to add at its C1 unit by the fold's §3.1 ES row for `paragon_employer`, CH its first consumer.
3. **A caught run** — a posted operative REFUSED at the target's town's gate, or TURNED BACK there by a patrol (§8) — writes the catching court's row at `confident`: the trail it carried leads home, and the exposure below fires once.
4. **The villain's own nature when held** (loyal — FIDELITY virtue — keeps silent; greedy — GENEROSITY vice — talks): WHEN ES-2b's custody wave LANDS; until then no engine holds a paragon, and what a villain the DM holds ("held at X", §12.4) gives up is the DM's word.

**Exposure — the SCANDAL on the one landed quantity.** When any court's row first reaches `confident` and the retainer carries no `exposedAt`, the hire is EXPOSED: the pass writes `retainer.exposedAt` — the LATCH, one blowback per hire, ever (`applyForeignExposureBlowback` adds resentment, a credibility charge and two court hits on EVERY call, so a row that stayed `confident` would otherwise re-fire weekly). The paragon stage calls the exported `corruptionWeb.js :: applyForeignExposureBlowback` with one source `{ foreign: true, settlementId: <the worked-against court>, patronId: <the employer>, kind: 'covert_hire_exposed', importance, name }` (`foreignExposuresFrom` already accepts the shape), so the landed triple fires: the `exposedCorruption[worked-against>employer]` entry (decaying war-reason fuel), the people-held grievance on the shared edge, a `deception` charge on the employer's CREDIBILITY STOCK (the one landed quantity for a court's standing at home and abroad, already read abroad by `pactFormation.js :: reserveFor`'s `OATHBREAKER_PENALTY * disavowal` — folded through `informationStatecraft.js :: advanceCredibility`, which returns unchanged unless `infoStatecraftActive`, so `infoStatecraftEnabled` is the blowback's credibility host flag; and it is ONE GLOBAL stock with no observer, `credibilityScoreOf(worldState, id, tick)`), and `corruption_exposed` on both courts — the pass applying the returned `courtHits` through the `settlementUpdates` RN's person stage threads to both returns (§4.1) (`activeConditions.js :: withActiveCondition`, the kernel's own applicator). The exposure is also an RN deed: its scandal lands on the members who did it (the ruler) as well as on the court (party brief §5). The function's ONLY landed caller is `pulseKernel.js` (`exposures: corruption.exposures || []`), and a CH source cannot join that list without a kernel or `npcAgency.js` (796/800) line, so the stage makes a SECOND call with its own exposures — the writer module stays `corruptionWeb.js` (`cure/L-CH.md` M11). That call IS CH's shared leaf `exposeCovertHire` (ONE IDIOM, THREE TIES, above), and THE BODIES' stage makes the same call for a body's tie, so a tick may carry the kernel's call, CH's and BD's: CH-7's first pin is a car-0 pin that every caller's call composes in one tick (each later call reads the earlier ones' `exposedCorruption`), and the source stays ONE word, `covert_hire_exposed`, for every tie.

**Accused before confirmed; the faked trail.** A court holding a `vague` or `uncertain` row is a court that has HEARD — the PAIR between it and the accused employer moves, and its dossier says so in its voice ("In Kell they say Merewick pays the agitator") — but only `confident` fires the blowback. A court's feeling about a court is the LANDED PAIR STATE, never RN (RN's refused pairs: "A holder `s:`/`c:` never holds an entry about a subject `s:`/`c:` … that feeling is the landed pair state"; R51 returns `null`, reason `pair_state`): each arrival that RAISES the court's band writes ONE typed incident on that pair through the one relationship writer — a STATE-ONLY patch, `relationshipEvolution.js :: applyRelationshipPatch(worldState, { relationshipKey, relationshipPatch: { trust: clamp01(t − m), resentment: clamp01(r + m) }, metadata: { incidentType: 'paragon_leak', subjectId, employerId }, proposalPayload: null }, now, edge)`, with `{ trust: t, resentment: r } = ensureRelationshipState(edge, worldState.relationshipStates?.[relationshipKey])`, the verdict writer's STATE idiom (PL §5.5; the landed precedent `sovereigntyTransfer.js :: accrueSaleGrievance`, which writes `resentment: clamp01(r + m)`, `trust: clamp01(t − m)` with `metadata: { incidentType: 'sovereignty_sale' }`) — `m` DRAFT 0.02, UNMEASURED; on the REAL graph edge between the two courts only (no edge ⇒ nothing is written: the grudge has nowhere to sit, the sale's own rule); at most two such incidents per court and hire (`vague`, `uncertain`), the blowback's own grievance at exposure the third and last. The writer appends the incident to the pair's `recentIncidents` (≤ 8, the landed shape). The applier's OWN read is the one used (`relationshipState.js :: ensureRelationshipState(edge, worldState.relationshipStates?.[relationshipKey])`, the very call `applyRelationshipPatch` makes for its `current`: "`const current = ensureRelationshipState(typedEdge, worldState.relationshipStates?.[outcome.relationshipKey]);`", CONFIRMED at `81f1cd963`), so a pair never yet materialized reads its type's defaults (`RELATIONSHIP_DEFAULTS`, a neutral pair's trust 0.45), never 0; the landed precedent's `Number(current?.trust) || 0` would collapse that trust to zero on the sale's first grievance — a LANDED defect in `sovereigntyTransfer.js :: accrueSaleGrievance`, carried in the fold's inbox for its owner — and CH cites the precedent for its SHAPE only. The incident's metadata names the hidden hand by `subjectId` (a paragon's `wnpc_` id or a body's id, the three ties above) and `employerId`. The two-step bound holds WHILE the court's row stands: a row forgotten (unrefreshed for `a_year`, or the oldest `vague` row evicted past four, L5) is a forgotten accusation, and a tale heard again after it is a new one. The TRUE bound is a RATE: one incident per court and hire per FRESH arrival, and fresh arrivals of one hire at one court come at most once per TTL (13 weeks — `mergeArrival` folds the rest, EXECUTED), so at most four a year at `m` 0.02, and none after the tie's latch (Exposure, above) or its end; no ratchet by repetition inside a TTL, and the pair state's own evolution carries the rest. A deceiver can fake the trail: a LIE record (IN's family; `liarId` = the deceiver's employer, never the free deceiver itself) naming an innocent court as employer, on a reopened `LURE_AXES` family `employer` — SLOT C1 (`infoStatecraftEnabled`, lit nowhere); the lie's exposure is IN's landed blowback on the liar.

**Tier.** The leak and the beliefs need a belief info mode (`beliefsActive`); the blowback needs `corruptionWebEnabled` (lit nowhere) and its credibility arm `infoStatecraftEnabled` (lit nowhere). OMNISCIENT ARM: where beliefs are off, no secret is modelled and none leaks — a covert retainer's secret is NEVER MODELLED: no leak, no accusation, no scandal, and its employer is NO GROUND OF REFUSAL at any town's gate (§11 step 3 reads a covert employer only through a `paragonBeliefs` row at `confident`, and none exists here), so the hire works as a retainer whose employer no court knows — the volume says so rather than exposing every hire at once. `corruptionWebActive` already requires `beliefsActive`, so the blowback is inert under omniscience; the arm is declared so that omniscience never reads as "every court knows". Degraded arm with beliefs on but the web dark: the rows and the dossier accusation exist; no blowback fires. A BODY's tie (§ ONE IDIOM) rides the same tier and adds THE BODIES' `bodiesEnabled` (its sponsorship and carriers their own flags, BD's); with CH dark (`paragonCovertEnabled` off) no belief row is written for ANY subject — so a lit BODIES with CH dark leaves an `employed` gang's secret unmodelled, said plainly, and THE BODIES' stage calls the leaf only where CH's covert arm is lit (BD's half). FIRST-VISIBLE: the first accusation line in a court's dossier; the exposure's Herald beat ("Merewick's hand behind the agitator in Kell is now beyond doubt; Kell's court calls it treachery").

## §10 RELATIONS — AUTHORED, EMERGENT, AND FELT NEARBY (CH-6)

**One regard idiom, RN's.** Every regard word in this volume is RN's `REGARD_BANDS` (`hatred · enmity · mistrust · indifference · fondness · esteem · adoration`) — the brief's `wary · neutral · warm · loyal · devoted` collide with landed words — and every fame word RN's `FAME_SCOPES` (`unknown · settlement · regional · realm`). Every relation a paragon holds or is held in is an RN regard link (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_RN.md`; party brief §5): sparse, capped, two-sided, decaying on the spine's banded stock toward neutral (emergent) or toward the authored baseline (authored), with typed reasons. CH owns no relation store. CH supplies: the AUTHORED baselines at introduction (`relations[]`, written through RN's `DECLARE_STANCE` door as `declared: <band>` — RN's widening of `declared` to a band baseline; never `dmSet`, which never decays and ignores every deed; emergent terms decay toward the authored band); the ACTS that make emergent links (served together, betrayed, refused at a town's gate, paid well, slandered, saved a town — each a deed on RN's deed ledger with RN's inherent value); and the PROXIMITY term.

**Proximity, foiled by personality (the owner's message 7).** When a candidate destination is in or near (`within_a_week` by `reachBandOf`, one hop aspatially) an actor the paragon relates to, the link enters the weekly score as ONE bounded term whose DIRECTION and STRENGTH are set by personality through §4.4's table: enmity makes a brave captain confront, a prudent one take the long road ("passing near Merewick, she took the long road around"), a vengeful one seek it out; love of a town makes a charitable saint linger and a paragon with high wanderlust move on regretfully. A bounded lean, never a compulsion; the DM may author or strike any relation (RN's door).

**Deities — the followers act.** No authored pantheon relation exists in code (R1 S30: the only inter-deity relation is `deityStance.js :: stanceOf`, derived from the two signed alignment axes). A saint's and a heretic's relation to another creed is that derived stance plus the CREEDS' emergent regard, written by their followers' history ("the faithful of the Lantern and the Ash have hated each other since the burning of Tolle"); it enters the saint's targeting (an opposed creed's ascendant town repels a cautious saint and draws a zealous one) and the town's-gate reason `opposed_creed` (§11), always through `stanceOf`'s CONSUMED fields (`cooperation`, `betrayalHazard`) or RN's creed regard — never `aggression`, an owner-gated build-ahead. An authored deity-relation field is CH-0c, the owner's hand, slotted beside CH-0b (§3; OD-CH-12).

**Fellowships and rivalries (proposal 7).** A fellowship is two paragons with mutual regard `fondness` or better whose own weekly choices land on the same leg: they travel together as a co-location (each still chooses alone each week); never a shared party object, never one actor — WC's product boundary 1 ("world-only; no rival adventuring parties") holds by construction. A rivalry is mutual regard at `enmity` or worse; between a champion and a villain of one domain it is a nemesis candidate (§13).

**Debts and favours (proposal 6).** RN regard incidents with typed reasons (`ransomed_by`, `paid_well`, `sheltered`, `betrayed`, `refused_at_gate`, `slandered`), owned by RN's closed reason vocabulary; a favour owed is a warm link with its reason, called in when the creditor court's offer weighs it (§7). CH'S REGISTRATION ROW — the three causes RN's `REGARD_CAUSES` lacked, appended by RN through this row ("PT/CH append rows by registration only", RN §2.4), each with its ONE door, holder and subject, written through RN's one regard writer only: `paid_well` — holder the paragon (`p:<wnpc>`; a corps `u:<unitKey>`), subject the employing court (`c:<court>`), written ONCE by CH's retainer leaf at an `accepted` `retainer_offer` verdict whose `termsBand` is `handsome` or `princely` (keyed by the engagement id, exact-once; DRAFT magnitude, UNMEASURED) — the favour a later offer from that court weighs (§7); `sheltered` — holder the paragon (or a free corps), subject the court of a member town that ADMITS it under its crown's ban (§11's harbouring), written ONCE by CH-5's harbouring write in the same act as its `fealty_defied` row (keyed by that row's `episodeId`, exact-once) — the town that took it in when its crown had barred it; `slandered` — holder the paragon, subject the LIAR court (`liarId`) of an IN lie that named the paragon as a hidden hand (the faked trail, §9), written when IN's landed blowback on the liar fires — DARK until IN's lie family reopens `LURE_AXES` `employer` (SLOT C1, `infoStatecraftEnabled`, lit nowhere), so until then nothing writes it and the word is registered for that door alone. `sheltered` is a HOMONYM of the landed `defensiveTerrain` band (§2.3's P5 row): the cause never prints bare — "Ashford gave her shelter when Merewick's crown had barred her". `ransomed_by` and `betrayed` are RN's own rows and doors (RN's half). WC's "cost memory" is no carrier of these: it has zero symbols and landed only as `contributionLedger.js`, a court-to-court war-contribution record that "WRITES NOTHING".

**The party.** §16.

**Tier.** RN's person regard needs `npcConsequencesEnabled`; until it lights, RN's regard is court-level only and CH is dark. FIRST-VISIBLE: the week a proximity choice is taken (its reason in the Herald beat or the dossier). Counterforce: decay toward neutral or the authored band; personality's direction; other terms.
