## §10b THE PATRON DEITY — A SWORN GOD; THE FOLLOWERS ACT, THE GOD DOES NOT (CH-11)

The owner's word, verbatim in THE OWNER'S WORDS above: "laslty, with the champion system champions and villains should also have a patron diety option that would also contribute to their decision makings". The design below was measured against the code of record first (`cure/L-CH-PD.md` §1, M1–M24).

**The field (OD-CH-51, the OWNER's persisted shape).** Any paragon — champion or villain, of any calling, a person or a corps (a corps lives "exactly as a paragon does", §16b) — MAY carry P1's `swornDeity`: a COMMITTED DEITY EMBED, built by the landed builder `deityCommitEmbed.js :: commitDeityEmbed(ref, snapshot)` (`_deityRef: ref, name, alignmentAxis, temperamentAxis, rankAxis, lawAxis, domain?, …authoredCharacterEmbedKeys(snapshot)` — "a stored record is always fully specified"), its identity `_deityRef` (`pantheon.js :: deityIdOf`). It is absent by default and written ONLY by the DM's `introduce` (first or revision, §3) through `paragonIntroduce.js`, from one of TWO sources: (a) a DM-authored custom-content deity (admitted by `customContentSchema.js :: validateDeity`), its ref minted by the ONE landed idiom `customRegistry.js :: mintDeityRef(raw)` ("`return scope ? \`deity:${scope}:${slug}\` : \`deity:${slug}\`;`", with the panel's own fallback `mintDeityRef(raw) || refId`) — the very ref a town seated by `DeityAssignmentPanel.jsx` takes, so step 5b's `patronRef === deityIdOf(embed)`, RN's `creed:<deityRef>` and the faith carrier meet ONE key (S69; the slug follows the god's name, so a renamed god is the DM's revision); (b) a god this campaign's world already carries — its STATE KEY verbatim and its world snapshot (`deitySnapshot.js :: worldFaithsForSave`'s idiom: "a restore must dispatch it as-is and never re-mint"). In a campaign born after 2026-07-28 every such god is DM-introduced (the deity doctrine: "Deities enter a world ONLY via custom-content introduction"; generation seeds none); a legacy save's pool-seeded god is that world's own lived history (THE PROMISE) and may be chosen (vetoable: the alternative offers source (a) only). Never premade, never engine-minted. The record CARRIES the embed rather than a bare id because the engine sees only embeds ("THE EMBED IS WHAT THE ENGINE CAN SEE … a field the embed does not carry is structurally invisible to the simulation", `deitySnapshot.js`): a bare reference to a god worshipped nowhere would read as absent (deities are keyed by REFS at master, never ids, so the field is no `patronDeityId`). A DM's later edit of the god changes no living paragon until the DM's revision re-copies it — the embed discipline every seated patron already lives by; and a god the DM DELETES from the library still leans its sworn one through the follower's own copy until the DM's revision clears or replaces the oath, while step 5b and the faith carrier simply find no town seating it. A LIBRARY paragon (CH-0b) carries its oath as the same self-contained embed, copied by `commitDeityEmbed` at its introduction — a cross-bucket deity reference CH-0b's manifest row must carry, the OWNER's hand with CH-0b. THE SAINT'S GOD IS ITS CREED — ONE CHOICE: for a faith-calling CHAMPION the oath is REQUIRED. The DM chooses the saint's god ONCE at the door; `introduce` writes `swornDeity` and `creedRef := deityIdOf(swornDeity)` from that one choice and refuses a saint with NO oath, or with a `creedRef` naming another god, with the receipt `sworn_creed_conflict` (a saint carrying one creed while sworn to another god would give one preaching two gods); `normalizeParagon` re-derives an AUTHORED faith champion's `creedRef` from its oath (an imported mismatch heals to the oath), so `creedRef` is the oath's copy kept by the one writer, never a second truth. The god then TRAVELS WITH ITS SAINT: §6.2's faith carrier (3a) hands `attemptEntry` the WORLD's own snapshot of the god's `_deityRef` wherever any religion state already holds one, and the oath's copy ONLY where none does — the one place the oath enters world state (`attemptEntry`'s seed makes the snapshot a town's religion-state snapshot, read by `deityRankStrength` and `faithField`), so a copy older than the DM's last edit never becomes a second snapshot of one god (CH22); the carrier needs the deity object, which an unsworn saint of an unseated god could not supply (`religiousContest.js`: "`const { deity, carrier, occupied } = reaching.get(dref);`"). Where no town seats any god the faith contest never runs (`religiousContest.js :: advanceReligionStates`: "`if (!isSubsystemActive(snapshot, 'religion')) return …`"; `religionActive` reads only the config's seated snapshots, `config?.primaryDeitySnapshot` / `cultDeitySnapshots`), and the deity doctrine makes entry share-driven and the patron seat sticky: a saint's oath to a god seated nowhere is ADMITTED AS CUSTOM CONTENT WITHOUT A CONTEST — the field stands (the oath is REQUIRED for a saint), the (3a) contest arm is DARK until the god holds a seat somewhere or the faith layer is active (`religionActive`), and a saint seats no god (vetoable). A HERETIC may be sworn to any god — the creed it works against included, which is the DM's FALLEN one and is priced below; a heretic's `creedRef` stays the creed it works against, never derived. A heard `polarity_turn` changes nothing of the field: a fallen saint stays sworn (OD-CH-55), and its authored `creedRef` — its god's — is now the creed it works against.

**The read.** `swornDeityOf(view) → DeityEmbed | null` is the one reader of the field (inside `paragonOf`'s view; §2.2's census admits no third dotted reader). The oath is PUBLIC unless the paragon's `secretKind` is `secret_faith`: a sworn paragon bears its god openly, so a court reads it at its own town's gate by PRESENCE — the paragon stands at its door, as its display noun does — and needs no belief row in any info mode. A court or a temple officer FAR from it knows the oath only as a PART of a telling it heard — carried with the doer as the display noun is, lost where the doer is lost (RN's part mask, RN's to rule) — never by reading the doer's live record. Under `secret_faith` no court reads it anywhere (step 5b and RN's route read the paragon as unsworn), `npcLedgerProjection.js`'s paragon audience arm (§2.4, no new line) shows it to the DM alone, and only the DM's revision reveals it — the spy system's `confirm` of it is ES's to offer at its C1 unit beside `paragon_employer` (the fold's §3.1 ES row); in every other audience that arm's non-DM allow-list carries the public oath ("sworn to <god>") beside the display noun — a one-entry widening of §2.4's row, no new line; `secret_faith` takes its first meaning here (OD-CH-54).

**The lean — ONE function, TWO named halves, inside the ONE roll (OD-CH-52).** `paragon/paragonSworn.js :: swornLean(view, { purpose, quarryKind, targetCreedRef }) → { act, align, bold, commit, reason }` — never RN's WHOLE reading one, whose kind term (zero for an evil holder: `K = max(0, 1 − 2·malice01)`) drowns its small force term, so a derived-WARLIKE god (at master every evil god: `deriveTemper`'s `W_EVIL: 0.7`) would lean its war champion LESS than a peacelike god's (EXECUTED: 1.0002 against 1.0235); the halves below are EXECUTED against those rows (`cure/D-CH-PD-r1-probe/lean2.out.txt`):
- THE GOD'S THREE COORDINATES (all landed reads of the embed): `lawfulness01 = 1 − chaos01(embed)`, `malice01 = evil01(embed)` (`deityAxes.js`: `evil: 1, neutral: 0.5, good: 0` · `chaotic: 1, neutral: 0.5, lawful: 0`), and the TEMPER WORD of the ONE temper seam `deityTemper(embed)` ("AUTHORED WINS … OTHERWISE DERIVE"). The INERT stored `temperamentAxis` is never read ("the DERIVED temper" of the carve-out is this one seam); `rankAxis` and every other key of the embed are never read (CH22). The landed derivation is the alignment restated — `deriveTemper`'s `W_EVIL: 0.7`, `W_CHAOS: 0.3`, `THRESHOLD: 0.15` make every evil god `warlike`, every good god `peacelike`, every neutral-alignment god `neutral` (EXECUTED) — so only an AUTHORED temper speaks apart from the alignment.
- THE ACT'S FACE: CH's OWN registered row (§2.2b — the one table RN's `inherentValueOf` derives from; never a second value) for the act the candidate would make: `work_here` and `answer_call`, and the exert decision → `paragon_exerted` for its domain on its LIVING side (`turned` over `authored.polarity`, §13); `pursue_nemesis` → `duel_won` on its side; `hunt_body` → `hunt_won` (the gang/beast row, or the conclave row when the quarry is a conclave). Every other purpose — `seek_work`, `take_rest`, `return_home`, `wander`, `visit_party`, `keep_rendezvous`, `court_errand` — makes no act, so `act` is exactly 1.
- THE TWO CARVED-OUT FACES: (1) the exert that answers a `captive_held` source reads the `captive_rescued` row RN registers from OP's proposal (OP §2.4), NEVER `paragon_exerted` — so the sworn lean weighs the rescue itself (a GOOD god's sworn frees a prisoner more readily; no recorded axis is "merciful"); (2) a paragon's `false_word` (OP §7.1) reads its OWN row on the DECEIVER's face (§2.2b), whatever its living side — a champion that lies does a villain's deed.
- THE ALIGNMENT HALF — the god's reading of the act: `align = 1 + SWORN_LEAN_WEIGHT × m` (DRAFT 0.10, UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster), `m` RN's reading of the act with the god as its holder — READING ONE with the god's force coordinate held at ½, so only its kindness and law terms speak (`lensOne(godCoords, face) / ((a + b) / 2)` over RN's own DRAFT weights, `m ∈ [−1, 1]` whatever they are tuned to; the temper is read ONCE, in the boldness half, never twice) — and, on a TARGET-DEPENDENT face (§2.2b's column: the faith exertion, the conclave hunt), mixed with RN's READING TWO at μ ½ (a god has no personality; RN's town default): the god's OWN creed helped reads +1 (a saint preaching its own god), its own creed hurt −1 (a fallen one working against it; a hunt of its own god's flock), and any other creed NO TIE (the act alone — the lean never reads a creed-to-creed stance; §7's values test does) (vetoable: the alternative reads `stanceOf` between creeds, a second creed-stance consumer inside the roll) — RN §6.2's "a holder the deed helped itself reads +1": read on the act's face alone, a saint of an evil creed would preach its own god LESS readily (EXECUTED 0.992); with the tie its alignment half reads at least 1.048 for every creed's saint (EXECUTED). The core and the mix are RN's to export as pure functions (`readDeed` keeps calling them — RN's to export; PLAUSIBLE until named) and CH never restates them — one reading law of an act by a character. So a good god's sworn finds kindness easier and cruelty harder; an evil god's shrugs at kindness (RN's own `K = 0`) and finds cruelty easier; a lawful god's finds lawless acts harder; and every sworn one favours its own faith's flock and recoils from a blow against it — the followers act, nothing is predicated of the god.
- THE BOLDNESS HALF — the god's temper against the act's force: `bold = 1 + ½·SWORN_LEAN_WEIGHT × sgn(force01 − ½) × T`, `T` = +1 for a `warlike`, 0 for a `neutral`, −1 for a `peacelike` temper — a warlike god's sworn finds a forceful act (the war calling, a duel, a hunt) easier and a gentle one (healing, preaching, a parley) harder, a peaceable god's the reverse; an act at force ½ (the wild's keeping, a trader's cheat, a false report) is untouched. It weighs HALF the alignment half, so the temper speaks where an act's morality is mild (the war deeds) and the god's morality speaks louder where the act's moral weight is strong (a plague spread) (EXECUTED both ways; vetoable: at equal weights an authored-warlike GOOD god's sworn plaguebearer would out-lean an evil god's). A warlike god's SAINT therefore preaches near its unsworn rate (`act` 0.996–1.012: its god's reading of its own preaching warm, `align` ≥ 1.048, its temper finding the gentle act less natural, `bold` 0.95), a neutral god's a little more readily (1.048–1.065) and a peaceable god's most (1.101–1.118) — EXECUTED; the two halves named apart in the Why panel.
- `act = align × bold` — MONOTONE PER HALF, each with the others held: `align` in malice and in law, `bold` (and so `act`) in an AUTHORED temper; on the DERIVED arm `deriveTemper` ties the temper to the alignment, so one malice step moves both halves and `act` can move AGAINST the face's kind coordinate (EXECUTED, 18 such rows: `cure/BRACKETS-B-champions-probe/s68i.rerun.out.txt`). Said plainly, an owner-visible consequence (OD-CH-52): under a derived temper a GOOD god's war champion leans BELOW an unsworn one (`act` 0.9722 against 1 — its alignment half 1.0233 warm, its boldness half 0.95) and below an evil god's (1.0535 under evil/lawful), and S68 (i) states only the rows the table supports. For every WAR DEED (the war calling's exertion either side, `duel_won` either side, `hunt_won` gang or conclave, its own god's flock included) EVERY warlike god's sworn leans MORE than EVERY peacelike god's, derived or authored (EXECUTED: the war champion 1.0465 under an evil, chaotic — derived warlike — god against 0.9722 under a good, lawful — derived peacelike — one). THE ENVELOPE, said once: by construction `act ∈ [0.855, 1.155]`; MEASURED over CH's registered faces × the 36 gods of the grid (three alignments × three laws × the derived and three authored tempers) `act ∈ [0.885, 1.120]`, the alignment half within [0.932, 1.067]. A god NEUTRAL in alignment, law AND temper leans no act by its character (both halves exactly 1 on a face with no tie — EXECUTED); its own flock's tie alone moves its saint's preaching (`align` 1.05) and its fallen one's betrayal (0.95). A god neutral in alignment and law with an AUTHORED temper leans ONLY through the boldness half, its alignment half exactly 1 (EXECUTED: `order/champion` 0.950, `order/villain` 1.050 under an authored-warlike neutral god).
- THE LAW HALF ON COMMITMENT: `commit = 1 + SWORN_LEAN_WEIGHT × (2·lawfulness01 − 1)` ∈ [0.90, 1.10], a multiplier on `COMMIT_MARGIN` (§4.3) — a lawful god's sworn keeps its aim and its bargain longer; a chaotic god's yields to a better offer at a smaller margin ("a chaotic god's villain reads no treaty as binding": its temporary retainer gives way sooner — never a forced break); exactly 1 for a god neutral in law (EXECUTED).
- WHERE IT ENTERS — one input of `paragonChoice.js :: weeklyChoice`'s own signature, never a second roll and never a new draw: (1) §4.4's score, each candidate × `act` (beside `habitFactor`); (2) §4.5's p(exert) × `act` for the exertion's face; (3) §4.3's `COMMIT_MARGIN` × `commit`; (4) §7's answer and `seek_work`'s values term — "its creed against the court's patron creed" reads, for a paragon of any calling, its sworn god by the SAME test (`stanceOf`'s consumed `cooperation` / `betrayalHazard`, or RN's creed regard) — one test, a wider holder.
- THE BOUND — THE FOLLOWER ACTS IN ITS OWN CHARACTER FIRST: `act` within [0.855, 1.155] and `commit` within [0.90, 1.10] lie inside a habit's span (±0.35, `habitCurve.js :: habitFactor`: `return 1 + span * tilt01;`, `HABIT_SPAN: 0.35`), and the `defining` row doubles its weight (§4.4), so a vengeful `defining` VILLAIN sworn to a good, lawful god — the most opposed reading of its duel in the whole grid (`act` 0.918, EXECUTED) — still pursues its nemesis over a rival call (S62). The lean never forbids an act and never compels one; the exert decision's keyed hash stays the one draw. The form is the landed one a deity's character already takes on a person (`corruption.js :: npcDeityDisfavor`: "a SINGLE bounded multiplier … Each multiplier lies in [1 − span, 1 + span] … NEUTRAL / absent deity ⇒ both EXACTLY 1.0") — bounded multipliers, each centred on 1.
- THE REASON: where `act` is among the three largest terms that raised the chosen aim, the reason word `keeps_the_faith` (§4.8) — "as her faith bids" on the page; never "her god wills"; on scrutiny the Why panel names the half that carried it in the follower's words ("her faith's mercy", "her faith's taste for the sword", "for her own faith's flock"), never the numbers and never a god's will.

**The welcome — the creed's step at the town's gate (§11 step 5b; OD-CH-53).** ONE step on the landed ladder between `admitted` and `admitted_under_watch`, never into or out of `refused` (a ban, the crown's ban, war, the faith calling's `opposed_creed` and renown are facts and judgments a faith never overrides): WARMER — `admitted_under_watch` → `admitted`, reason `patron_creed` — where the paragon's sworn god holds THIS town's patron seat (`religionStates[town].patronRef === deityIdOf(embed)`) and ONLY over the watch step 5 set (the WATCH's `mistrust`): a watch the crown's ban set at step 2 (a `shaky` hold) stands, for a faith does not wash a crown's word, and never for a paragon the court believes a villain (step 5's second arm: a villain's faith does not wash its deeds); COLDER — `admitted` → `admitted_under_watch`, reason `suppressed_creed` — where its god's creed is `suppressed` in this town (WF-1b's one writer: "WHEN THE BELIEVERS' CREED WAS PUT OUT OF THE LIGHT" by an eviction, a capacity contest or a siege) or, where THE BODIES are lit, this court holds that creed's flock `outlawed` (BD §7.1's word; BD's creed-keyed read, BD's to name; PLAUSIBLE). The ladder's MIDDLE — a creed at `cult` or `established`, or not present — moves nothing (the brief's "the creed's standing", read at its two ends; vetoable). Both reads are the deciding court's OWN facts (truth — §2.2 `gateDefault`'s rule), the oath a presence fact. A step mints a card only as the landed rule does (`admitted_under_watch` is heard; a plain admission is a dossier line).

**The flock — RN's (CH writes no regard).** The faithful's regard of a paragon (`creed:<ref>`, "a creed's faithful — a body (the followers act; a god holds nothing)", RN §2.2) is DERIVED from that creed's temple officers (RN §5.3). RN's route: a temple officer of creed X reading a deed whose HEARD telling carries the doer's oath to X (the oath a part of the telling, above — never `swornDeityOf` on the live record) takes reading one with X's recorded coordinates — the followers judge their sworn one by their god's character (the 09-12 carve-out; BD4's idiom for conclaves); this OVERRIDES RN §6.2's declared "a person its own" character for one doer class, REGISTERED in RN §6.2's "Which character each holder reads" as the one override of "a person its own" (reading one reads the god's coordinates from the TELLING's carried oath, never a live read; the god-to-coordinate mapping is this volume's, THE GOD'S THREE COORDINATES above, per half; RN's exports PLAUSIBLE until RN-3's car-0) — so a deed in the god's character is remembered warmly and a deed against it becomes a grudge (the counterforce); no new cause word (RN's `heard_deed`). There is NO credibility cost: credibility weighs a person's WORD (RN §7: "A paragon's credibility is the landed `npcCredibility` holder for its `wnpc_`"), and a deed against one's god is no false word — the brief's "credibility/regard cost" keeps its regard half alone. Until RN lands the route, the flock reads a sworn paragon as any doer.

**The paragon's own counterforce — shame.** An `exerted` week whose face its own god's ALIGNMENT reading condemns (`m ≤ −½` — the alignment half's `m`, its own flock's tie included; the bar DRAFT on the FP-46 roster) raises `shame` one step — §4.5's landed mood on `bandedStock.decayTowardNeutral`, half-life `a_season`, no new decay law — so the fallen saint's weeks weigh on it. REACHED (EXECUTED, `cure/D-CH-PD-r1-probe/lean2.out.txt` T5): by the fallen saint's exertion against its own creed under every GOOD god and under a neutral lawful or a wholly neutral one (`m` −0.583 at most under a good god); by the plaguebearer's under a good lawful or good neutral god; by the wildland villain's under a good lawful god. NOT reached — and said so: by a gentle act under a warlike god (the boldness half is no judgment), and by a fallen saint of an EVIL creed (its god's reading one admires the cruelty the betrayal wears; its flock's tie alone stops short of the bar) (beyond the brief's list; vetoable: the alternative leaves the cost to the flock's regard alone).

**What it never does.** No miracle, no divine intervention, no omen the engine mints, no blessing stat, no theology; no god acts, speaks, wills, rewards or punishes; the engine never writes a religion state or a pantheon entry, and never a deity embed beyond the DM's door's own copy (`paragonIntroduce.js`) and the normalizer's fixed-point re-pick of that copy (`paragonRecord.js` — EXECUTED a fixed point, `cure/D-CH-PD-r1-probe/repick.out.txt`) (S67); the embed's boon and bane channels ride with the oath and are read by nothing (`faithField.js` reads them on a town's religion-state snapshot, never on a paragon's record); a sworn paragon pays no tithe (CH10; the tithe is the church's one flow, WF §3.1.5); a sworn saint's gift is still §6's — the lean moves WHETHER it exerts, never what an exertion does.
These are CH's OWN reads and writes: the ONE door by which an oath enters WORLD state is (3a)'s `attemptEntry` seed (§6.2) — the contest's own writer seeds the religion state, CH writes none — taking the world's own snapshot of the ref wherever one exists and the oath's copy only where none does; the seeded state's `boonChannel` and `rankAxis` are then the contest's reads of a town's religion state (CH22).

**Counterforces (requirement 2).** The bound (below habits and the defining trait); the flock's regard (RN's route); `shame`; the creed's standing moving under the faith ladder — a god put down in a town makes its sworn one's welcome there colder, the slow sign (requirement 16); habits still the carrier; the DM's hand (revise or clear the field; each hearing's three choices).

**Clock (requirement 9).** Weekly, inside the one roll; the creed's step at each arrival; the field changes only by the DM's revision. FIRST-VISIBLE: the week a sworn paragon arrives at a town that seats its god or has put it down (the town's-gate line), or the first week `keeps_the_faith` is among an aim's three reasons (the paragon page).

**Surface · scrutiny · machinery (CH19).** SURFACE: "sworn to the Lantern" beside the display noun on the paragon's card and in its Herald lines (absent under `secret_faith`); the town's-gate card's reason in words. SCRUTINY: the paragon page names its god and that god's recorded character in words (good or evil, lawful or chaotic, and the temper word — read through `swornTemperOf`, never a `deityTemper` call of the page's own — a second caller would red the landed census walker) and `keeps_the_faith` among an aim's reasons, with the half that carried it. MACHINERY: the coordinates, `m`, the halves and the weights — never shown.

**Couplings — one direction, through CH's one roll (the brief's item 5).** OP: a paragon's opportunity choice reads `weeklyChoice` with the lean inside; OP never reads a deity (OP19). OP's sentence "a lawful god's envoy-champion never plants a false report" holds by FACE — no HEROIC-FACED act is a false report — for any paragon may take OP's `false_word` through the one roll (the owner's 08:41 "Envoys, champions, spies, etc could also provide false information"; the call-board half at §14 and §4.2), registered on the deceiver's (villain-side) face (§2.2b's `false_word` row), shameful: a champion that lies does a villain's deed, the DM's to mark. Of any sworn one the lean says "less readily" — of a lawful god's deceiver, less readily than a chaotic god's of the same good-evil axis (the law half is monotone, S68) — never "never", and an evil, lawful god's deceiver still lies a little more readily than an unsworn one (EXECUTED, `cure/D-CH-PD-r1-probe/lean2.out.tsv`); its sworn lean reads whatever face §2.2b registers for it, like any act (`cure/L-OP.md` §5's CH route). MG: a commanding paragon's verbs and motives are named from its aim through the same roll; MG never reads the deity (`cure/L-MG.md` §5's route). WS: a court's way of war reads the COURT's own faith temper (`courtTemperOf`), never a champion's sworn god. RN: the reading-one core and the reading-two mix exported, and the sworn-doer reading on the heard oath (RN's). WF: the seat (`patronRef`), the standing and `suppressed` READ, never written; the tithe untouched. BD: BD4's idiom cited; the creed-keyed `outlawed` read BD's. W-LIVES: a sworn god's pantheon fortunes may reach its sworn one only through a NEW join rung `swornDeity` (an EVENT heard through the ONE drift law — the `god_fortunes_rose` / `god_fortunes_fell` adapters over `realmEvents.js`'s `impactKind: 'pantheon_ascendancy'` and its twilight twin), never a widening of the landed `'patronDeity'` rung, which keeps its SETTLEMENT-patron sense (`subjectsByPatronDeity`: "Every roster of every settlement whose patron deity is the named one"), and never a read of the pantheon rank — W-LIVES's unit, dark until `characterDriftEnabled`. ES: the `secret_faith` confirm kind (ES's, C1). PT: a sworn paragon meets the party only through the roll (no PT change).

**Tier.** The roll's lean with `paragonsEnabled` (§7's values term with `paragonRetainersEnabled`); the creed's step with `paragonGateEnabled`; the flock with RN's `renownEnabled` and RN's route; a deity-bearing world for the faith CONTEST (a deity-free world, and every born world until the DM seats a god — WF: "the birth preset seats no deity" — runs no contest, so (3a) is dark there, while the field stands on the record and leans the paragon's own roll: the roll's lean and the page read it — vetoable, the alternative keeps the lean dark too until the god is seated; a saint needs a god the DM has made — its oath is required — and its oath to a god seated nowhere is admitted as custom content WITHOUT a contest, (3a) dark until the god holds a seat somewhere or `religionActive`). NO NEW FLAG (vetoable: a seventh flag would let a lit world switch the lean off without clearing the DM's field). Aspatial-safe. OMNISCIENT ARM: identical at the town's gate (the oath a presence fact, never a belief row); a far hearer in the omniscient arm knows the deed with all its parts at once (RN's projected scope), the oath among them.

---

## §11 THE TOWN'S GATE AND THE BANS (CH-5)

**The town's gate — `person_at_the_gate`, through ONE leaf.** When a paragon's leg arrives at a settlement where it is not resident or retained — BEFORE any lodging write (§4.3) — the leaf `paragon/gateDefault.js :: gateDefault({ worldState, snapshot, graph, actorRef, settlementId, tick })` (`graph` the regional graph the person stage already receives, `regionalGraph` — the pair-label reads of step 3 need it) — PT's `party_gate` passes the same graph (PT's half) computes the default for every actor kind (a person here; PT's `party_gate` calls the same leaf with `{kind:'party'}`), and the WATCH keeps the town's gate (RN's `actingOfficer(worldState, settlementId, 'refusal at the gate')`; the ruler where the office is empty; the officer's own regard and reason ride the card). The order is fixed, with ONE reason vocabulary for every actor kind — never `admissionFor`, the rehost economy's faction-SEATING admission (outcomes `admitted_lowest · founded_sibling · rejected · remained_roaming`), which keys belief on `originRef.settlementId`, a field a DM-introduced paragon lacks: the town's gate composes the ban read and `rejectionFor`'s table instead (`cure/L-CH.md` M10):
1. **The ban first, from truth** — `isExcludedFrom(worldState, <actor key>, settlementId, tick, EDICT_EXCLUSION_KINDS)` ("The edict is a fact about the door, not an opinion about the person") ⇒ `refused`, reason `banished_from_here`.
2. **The crown's ban** — `polity_ban`: a polity-wide ban proclaimed by the root's BENCH, read at a member's town's gate by the MEMBER's believed hold (PL's `polityHold` band `frayed · shaky · staunch · unbroken`) and only where PL's `crownReachAt(…).share01` at that member is above zero (PL keeps each consumer's threshold in that consumer's volume — these are CH's): `staunch` or `unbroken` ⇒ `refused`; `shaky` ⇒ `admitted_under_watch`; `frayed` ⇒ no weight (the dossier notes the crown's word unheeded); the PROCLAMATION itself is read as the crown's public word at its members' doors (truth, the one named exception of §2.2 `gateDefault` — an edict proclaimed to members is public as a declared relationship is) and only the HOLD that weighs it through the member's belief — a `frayed` hold's admission writes no fealty row, for the crown's word no longer reaches that town's gate — the thresholds stated ONCE, here, for persons and for the party.
3. **Circumstance, from belief** — `under_quarantine` (the court believes the plague here or where the actor came from is `grave` or worse — ITS READ NAMED ONCE: "here" is the deciding court's OWN town, its own fact read as truth (§2.2 `gateDefault`'s rule); "where the actor came from" — the settlement its arriving leg departed — is read ONLY through the deciding court's own CALL BOARD (§14): a `health` call at that town at `grave` or `ruinous` as this court KNOWS it (presence, a telling's arrival or a belief band; the truth only in the omniscient arm), and where its board holds no such call the origin half does not fire — never a truth read of another town's plague, for which `beliefMap.js`'s `conditionsBands` ("believed {pullBand, routePositionBand, storesBand, tierBand}") carry no carrier; PG pg-4's quarantine or gate-closure verb is DESIGNED ONLY and quarantine is an owner value: when it is built as the host's own verb with a stance input (one stance vocabulary `open · taxed · restricted · closed` with EC-6 and PS), this reason reads that stance at the town's gate for people instead of the believed-severity proxy; until then the proxy stands, never a patrol fact), `at_war_with_employer` (a person: its employer — as the court believes it, so a covert employer counts only once believed; the party: its backer of record, PT's `backing.by` — stands at open war with this court: a live war front between them, `roads/embassyHazard.js :: atOpenWar(graph, courtId, servedId)`, `servedId` the court the actor serves — the retainer's employer, the party's backer), `opposed_creed` (the faith calling: a saint or heretic whose creed this town's faithful regard at `enmity` or worse in RN, or whose creed's stance toward this town's patron — where that patron stands `ascendant` — reads low `cooperation` or high `betrayalHazard` on `stanceOf`'s CONSUMED fields; `aggression` is never read), `enemy_in_service` (ONE meaning for every actor kind: the actor serves — a retainer for a person, a backing for the party — a court whose LANDED PAIR LABEL with this court is `hostile` AS THIS COURT BELIEVES IT, with no live front: `beliefMap.js :: readBeliefRelationship(courtId, servedId, worldState, relationshipTypeBetween(graph, worldState, courtId, servedId)) === 'hostile'` — the believed (and possibly STALE) `allianceLabel` where the court holds a belief record of the served court, the truth type `roads/embassyHazard.js :: relationshipTypeBetween` only as that landed read's own fallback ("ground truth verbatim (dormant / self / unknown — the declared relationship is public, so absence is NON-paranoid)"), the same call the chooser makes (`settlementStrategy.js`: "`readBeliefRelationship(id, other, worldState, relState.relationshipType)`") — never RN, because a court's feeling about a court is the pair state) ⇒ `refused`. THE SERVED COURT is itself read as this court believes it, for both reasons: an OPEN retainer's `employerId`, the party's backer of record (`backing.by`, public by the act); a COVERT retainer's employer only where this court's `paragonBeliefs` row names it at `confident` or `certain` (`believedEmployerOf`, P3) — a `vague` or `uncertain` row is an accusation (§9), never a ground of refusal, so a hidden hire is never exposed at a town's gate by the truth — in the omniscient arm no such row exists, so a covert employer is no ground of refusal there either (§9 Tier; S8) (the signature read at `81f1cd963`: `readBeliefRelationship(observerId, subjectId, worldState, truthType)`; PT's P49 pins it, PT's half). The two reasons together are the landed `atWarWith` ("`atOpenWar(graph, a, b) || relationshipTypeBetween(graph, worldState, a, b) === 'hostile'`" — the ruling's "`hostile`, or at war") with its RUNG half read through belief (above) and its FRONT half the court's own war; `at_war_with_employer` is read first and the reason word names which half fired — the war front to `at_war_with_employer`, the `hostile` label to `enemy_in_service`, so the two words never name the same fact (vetoable: the alternative strikes one word, which PT's card also cites) ; both halves are open war in the roads' sense (`atWarWith`'s own comment: "a live war-layer front OR a `hostile` relationship rung (§4: 'hostile = open war')"), and the two words name WHICH sign of it the court reads — `at_war_with_employer` a live front between the two courts, `enemy_in_service` the declared `hostile` rung with no front yet — so the card says "at war with" for the first and "sworn enemy of" for the second, never "at peace".
4. **Renown, from belief** — a person: `npcCirculationTable.js :: rejectionFor({ faction: <the governing faction>, believed, factionAlignmentRead })` with `believed` = RN's believed fame and moral read of the paragon PROJECTED onto the facets (`notorietyBand` from fame × the regard's moral side, `scandalClass` from its deeds' registered moral weight — §2.2b, `alignmentRead` from its believed alignment): `scandal_class_unacceptable · alignment_opposed · notoriety_too_loud` ⇒ `refused`; the party: PT's `netEffectAt` at `enmity` or worse ⇒ `refused`. The notoriety projection into this step is RN's `renownLeansEnabled` (RN §19); without it the step is skipped.
5. **Watch** — no refusal fired, but the WATCH's regard (the court's where persons are dark) is `mistrust`, or it believes the actor a villain ⇒ `admitted_under_watch`.
5b. **The creed's step** — for a SWORN paragon (a person or a corps) whose oath this court can see (not `secret_faith`): ONE step between `admitted` and `admitted_under_watch`, never into or out of `refused` — WARMER, reason `patron_creed`, where its god holds THIS town's patron seat (`religionStates[town].patronRef`), the court does not believe it a villain, and the watch is the one step 5 set (the WATCH's `mistrust`) — a watch the crown's ban set at step 2 (a `shaky` hold) stands; COLDER, reason `suppressed_creed`, where its god's creed is `suppressed` here or, THE BODIES lit, this court holds that creed's flock `outlawed`; both reads the court's own facts (truth); the party's and a body's arms take no step (they swear no oath). On THE CORPS ARM (below) the step runs between that arm's (5) and (6), reading the company's mark's `swornDeity`; that arm's "it has no creed" (its step 3) means the company carries no `creedRef` — the `opposed_creed` reason, never the oath.
6. Otherwise `admitted`.

A hearing is minted only when the default is `refused` or `admitted_under_watch`; a plain admission is not an engagement (the MN precedent: "Raids are not hearings … Otherwise one menace would flood the docket every week"). Verdicts `admitted · refused · admitted_under_watch`. `admitted_under_watch` is a DERIVED wariness bump on ES's host-side catch odds (`WARINESS_W`) for the stay, carried by the hearing seal, never a saved status; degraded arm (ES dark): the watch is the dossier line alone. A refused paragon reroutes, waits in the nearest neighbour (its `residency` there), or, by its own nature, tries another way in only where a BAN stands (below).

**A harbour passed at sea is no arrival — a coast voyage is ONE leg, port to port.** A paragon's ordinary leg is ONE hop and lands at its far end (`npcCirculationTransit.js :: nextHopToward` takes `path[1]` of the cheapest candidate route; `advanceWanderer`: "arriving ⇒ land at the far end and CLOSE the leg, taking no further hop this tick"). When its next hop LEAVES ITS PORT BY SEA, CH's road leaf `paragonRoad.js` plans the VOYAGE instead: over the cheapest route `candidateRoutes(digest, fromId, destId, 1)` it reads the hops' modality by the landed `seaRoads.js :: classifyLegModes(digest, path)` (`land · sea`; the read PS2 uses) and runs forward over the consecutive SEA hops to the first node where the paragon LANDS — the aim's destination, or a port whose next hop leaves by land or by a teleport edge — and opens ONE leg through the one physics, `namedPersonTransit.js :: openNamedPersonLeg({ fromId: <embarkation port>, toId: <landing port>, departTick: tick, nominalWeeks: hopWeeks(digest, <embarkation port>, <landing port>, season) })`, priced by the one read (`pathCost` takes the cheapest sea-augmented route between the two ports, its storm season included). One leg spanning several nodes is a landed idiom (`namedPersonPathPosition` projects one leg onto a caller-owned route, which Roads and the sea roads already use), and the leg keeps the closed H3/J4 shape — no path is stored. Harbours passed at sea are NOT leg ends: nothing arrives there, so no town's gate is read, nothing lodges and no ban fires, and the landed "taking no further hop this tick" is untouched; mid-voyage the paragon stands at no settlement (`namedPersonLegPosition`: "explicitly not at either settlement"), and a sea hop has no half and no watch (PR §2.2). The LANDING ends the leg, the town's gate reads it as any arrival, and the next leg opens the next tick. A teleport arrival and every land arrival are the town's gate's as before. EXECUTED (`cure/D-CH-r3-probe/voyage.out`, an eight-port coast fixture with sea lanes, both seasons): in 72 voyages that pass a harbour at sea, the one leg is never at a settlement mid-voyage, never slower than the landed hop-by-hop walker and at least one week long, while the hop-by-hop walker, which re-plans from each port it reaches, arrives at an intermediate node in every voyage (156 intermediate arrivals) — the behaviour this rule removes. The corps arm meets a town's gate only where WC's stepper LANDS it (WC-10).

**The corps arm (a FREE company at a town's gate).** THE PASSAGE rules so: a free corps "meets a town through CH's gate (the corps arm of `person_at_the_gate`), never this ladder" (PS1 (d), PS §5.5, OD-PS-34). A corps with NO standing retainer (PS's "free") that arrives at a settlement meets the same leaf with `actorRef = { kind: 'corps', id: unitKey }`, the same order and the same reasons; a corps carrying a retainer — bound, or free under a temporary one (PS's "retained") — is its employer's unit: THE PASSAGE judges it on foreign land, and at its destination it is its employer's force, never a town's-gate case. The steps: (1) the ban from truth, on the edge keyed `u:<unitKey>` (PT's SHAPE 3: the actor-ref widening's key grammar `wnpc_…` · `party` · `u:<unitKey>`, "which CH writes" — the BENCH's emergent ban and the DM's `banish` write a company's edge as a person's); (2) `polity_ban`, as for a person; (3) of the circumstance reasons only `under_quarantine` (an unretained company serves no court, so `at_war_with_employer` and `enemy_in_service` cannot fire, and it has no creed); (4) renown: the WATCH's regard of the company (RN, subject `u:<unitKey>`) at `enmity` or worse ⇒ `refused` — `rejectionFor`'s person table is never read for a unit; (5) the watch: `mistrust`, or it believes the company of the villain side ⇒ `admitted_under_watch`; (6) otherwise `admitted`. A refused company does not quarter there: WC's one stepper keeps it where it stood, and its next weekly choice weighs another destination; on a road a patrol's turn-back reads the same words (§5). The card is `person_at_the_gate` with the corps as its party — ONE kind for both holder kinds of the mark (`person · corps`), its name kept because PR8, PS1 (d) and the fold's §3.4 already cite it (§2.3's register row). Lands with CH-10 (WC-10).

**The road arm with a BODY as its counterparty (THE BODIES §9.5).** When a body and a paragon on its leg MEET — THE PATROLS' one encounter law with the body as the watcher (BD §9.1 widens it; PR §8.1) on the road `road (a, b)` or on a way they share (`trail:<a|b>`, THE BODIES' key on the one ladder, §5) — and the body's OWN week chooses to stop the traveller (a hostile stance and a nature that strikes; BD's choice, BD §4), the engagement is CH's road arm: ONE `person_at_the_gate` card whose party is the body (`{ kind: 'body', id: <bodyId> }`, the sixth actor kind), WHERE `road (a, b)` — the way named on the card when they met on one (the body's half, BD20; the fold's WHERE row: "THE TRAIL IS NO SIXTH WHERE KIND"; a named junction `junction:<id>` a key under it, never a kind), verdicts narrowed to `admitted · refused` (a body keeps no watch, so `admitted_under_watch` is not offered), default `refused` — the paragon TURNED BACK: it reroutes or waits where its leg began, and its next week chooses again. NOTHING ELSE CAN HAPPEN TO A PARAGON AT A BODY'S HAND — save the attempt on a WARDEN below, whose verdicts are as harmless to his record: no seizure (custody waits on ES-2b, §12.4; L-BD 34), no death (CH3), no robbery (no person holds coin, CH10) — and a paragon never FIGHTS a body alone: its strength against a body is the unit it rides with (a hunt it leads beside THE PATROLS' detachment — `patrol_engages`, PR §11.2; a corps' own band, §16b), its gift a lean on that unit (`paragonLean`), never a second battle law (CH5). A body that LETS a paragon pass writes no card (a plain admission is never an engagement). A paragon meeting a body it LEADS is no meeting. TIER: `paragonGateEnabled` + THE BODIES' `bodiesEnabled` (the trail half `hiddenWaysEnabled`); dark ⇒ bodies meet no paragon. This narrowing and "never fights alone" widen the one road-arm kind; no new kind is minted (vetoable: the alternative mints a CH kind for a lone paragon's skirmish with a body, `passed · turned_back · fought`, and a lone champion's band — a second contest the house does not have).

**The attempt on a WARDEN — the road arm's second card (THE BODIES §9.5 "A WARDEN IN THE WAY", L-BD 41 (b)).** The ruling of record on the owner's word of THE BODIES (L-BD 41): "a gang may bribe or kill the warden — a deed through CH's road arm". A town whose public-security office is held by a WARDEN (BD §2.3's office read: a paragon by `securityOfficeOf` + `isWardenParagon`, or a non-paragon office-holder on the person ledger whose roster role is `warden`) is more dangerous to a body, so a NAMED gang's own week may choose to strike at him (BD's `bribe` choice where his wages go unpaid — THE PATROLS' item-16 slot; or an attack — BD's choice, BD §4): the engagement is ONE `person_at_the_gate` card (no new kind) whose party is the gang and whose subject is the WARDEN (`{ kind: 'person', id: <wnpcId> }`), WHERE `road (a, b)` where they met on his leg (the way named on the card) or `settlement` — his town — where the gang came to him, in the attempt's own week (requirement 24); verdicts `withstood · bribed`, DEFAULT `withstood` (nothing has happened to the warden: the attempt failed or was turned away); `bribed` is OFFERED only where his wages go unpaid (PR's item-16 slot; a PARAGON's terms read as paid until W-COIN's `retainers` row, CH10, so for a paragon warden it is never offered until W-COIN lights), and its effect is THE BODIES' `bribe` row's alone — the court's stance toward the gang moves one step toward `tolerated` at that court's next `body_stance` fork (BD §4.2) — never a coin to a person (CH10), never a change to his record; the warden's DEATH is NEVER a verdict and never the engine's (CH3; the owner's "no named fate"): the DM who wants him dead says so with his own `kill` (a ledger person's landed `killNamedNpc`), outside the card. The act is ONE deed, `warden_attempted` (§2.2b), minted by THIS card's close through RN's writer — the hearing's owner mints — its doer the gang (`g:<bodyId>`), its target the warden, whatever the verdict; a nameless gang strikes at no warden (no doer key, RN; THE BODIES' naming band). Long advances run it on its default (`withstood`). TIER: `paragonGateEnabled` + THE BODIES' `bodiesEnabled` (the persons the office reads lit, `npcConsequencesEnabled`). The attempt is an arm of the one road-arm kind (the fold's count unchanged), its subject any warden on the person ledger, its verdicts the two the house can carry without a fate (vetoable: the alternative re-points the attempt to a kind of THE BODIES' own, and CH keeps "NOTHING ELSE CAN HAPPEN" with no exception).

**Bans — one ban, the landed edge.**
- **Authored** — the DM's `banish` verb (a `DM_VERBS` amendment beside `introduce`; inverse in `undoDmVerb`) or the definition's `bans[]` at introduction: `banishment_edict` edges with `indefinite: true`, canon until the DM's `pardon`. THE INVERSE RESTORES THE PRIOR EDGE EXACTLY: `banish`'s undo token stores the edge that stood at that door before it — windowed (an emergent ban, with its `untilTick`), indefinite, or none — and `undoDmVerb`'s `banish` arm lifts the door's `banishment_edict` edge through `liftExclusionEdges(…, { settlementId, kinds: ['banishment_edict'] })` and, where a prior edge stood, re-adds it through `addExclusionEdge` — the ONE exclusions writer, the same replay master's own `pardon` inverse makes ("pardon: re-shut every door, with its original window", `npcDmVerbs.js :: undoDmVerb`); never "lift the edges it wrote", because after the strictest-wins merge (`normalizeExclusionEdges`: one edge per door and kind, "already the strictest") the DM's edge and the prior one ARE one edge. AND THEN THE COURT'S OWN WINDOW: after the prior edge is restored, the inverse RE-WRITES the window the door's `banKeys` row carries (`untilTick`, P2) through the same `addExclusionEdge` WHERE IT IS STILL AHEAD — so a court's ban minted AFTER the DM's `banish` at that door, which the strictest-wins merge folded into the DM's indefinite edge, STANDS through the DM's undo (the court's sentence was its own act, never the DM's), and a key whose window has passed by the undo drops with nothing re-written (PT §6's R4-1 rule for `BAN_PARTY`, one rule on two records). The prior emergent ban's `banKeys` key, kept INERT while the DM's word stood (below), is live again the week the windowed edge returns (MODELLED over master's one writer, `cure/D-CH-r5-probe/banKeysWindow.out`: the reverse order re-writes the window — banned at 150, open at 230; the forward order, S45, restores the prior window exactly — banned at 250, open at 310; a window passed by the undo re-writes nothing and drops the key; no prior and no key leaves the door open; a prior authored indefinite edge stands; a re-ban leaves one key, the newer episode, the stricter window (300); without the re-write the court's later ban is LOST, `cure/D-CH-r5-probe/banishThenVerdict.rerun.out`; S45b pins the reverse order) — the rule `BAN_PARTY`'s undo follows too (PT), EXECUTED on master's one writer (`cure/D-CH-r4-probe/banKeysUndo.out`: a windowed ban at C, then the DM's `banish` at C — the naive inverse leaves C unbanned, `edgeAtC: null`, the cured inverse restores `{ untilTick: 300 }` exactly and a seat change then lifts it; with no prior edge the inverse leaves none, with a prior indefinite edge it leaves that edge).
- **Emergent** — the court's BENCH decides (RN's `actingOfficer(worldState, settlementId, 'ban proclaimed')`; the ruler where the office is empty — RN §5.3's dark person arm): when a deed it has heard of harmed it (the act's registered inherent value, §2.2b, read as the officer reads it) and the officer's regard of the paragon falls to `enmity` or worse, it proposes a `person_banned` hearing (`banned · not_banned`; default `banned` at `hatred`, otherwise by the officer's personality — a vengeful judge bans, a forgiving one does not; the card names the officer and the reason). A ban minted by the hearing is a WINDOWED edge whose `untilTick` is DERIVED AT MINT from the regard band (`hatred` → `a_few_years`, `enmity` → `a_year`, by `bandedStock.js :: HALF_LIFE_WEEKS`) — the owner's "emergent bans LAPSE over time" with no second decay law and no edge-shape change.
- **Lifted** — the DM's `pardon` (`liftExclusionEdges`); and a court's own pardon: when that court's seat changes hands, the windowed edges IT minted lapse at the next weekly pass through the same lift (the owner's "a new ruler at a succession"). THE READ is PL's ONE shared `seatChangedSince(worldState, settlementId, since)` over both landed tails (`powerStructure.previousGovernments` — coups, conquest, appointment, the DM's `CHANGE_RULING_POWER` — and `seatTransitions`), in its EXACT KEY FORM: when a `person_banned` verdict mints a windowed edge, the pass records the banning court's `seatEpisodeKeyOf(worldState, courtId)` and the edge's window beside it on the banned paragon's own record (`paragon.banKeys: { settlementId, episode, untilTick }`, ≤ 8 windowed; an `ExclusionEdge` carries no mint tick — `{ settlementId, kind, untilTick }` — and its shape is not changed; `untilTick` the stricter of the door's emergent windows, as the landed merge keeps it); each week, where the banning court reads `seatChangedSince(…, episode).changed === true`, the edge at that door is lifted ONLY IF IT IS STILL WINDOWED, and its key drops with it (a key also drops when its edge lapses or is pardoned); a key whose door now stands INDEFINITE is INERT — kept, outside the cap, lifting nothing at a seat change — and drops with its edge (the DM's `pardon`) or once its own `untilTick` passes (PT §2.1's rule), so an undone `banish` (above) finds it live again and re-writes its window: where the landed strictest-wins merge (`npcLedgerFacets.js :: normalizeExclusionEdges` keeps one edge per (settlementId, kind), and an `indefinite` edge beats any window) has folded the emergent ban into the DM's own `indefinite` one, the DM's ban stands and the seat change lifts nothing at that door — the DM's word is lifted only by `pardon` (EXECUTED, `cure/D-CH-r3-probe/banLapse.out`: a windowed ban alone lifts; the emergent ban merged under the DM's `banish` lifts nothing under the guard and stays banned, while an unguarded door-scoped lift would erase the DM's ban). ONE KEY PER DOOR: a re-ban at the same door REPLACES its key with the newer episode (the landed merge keeps one edge per door and kind, so two keys for one edge would be two lift rules for one fact). THE CAP COUNTS WINDOWED KEYS ONLY: at a ninth windowed key the OLDEST WINDOWED key evicts — its edge stays and lapses at its own `untilTick`, losing only the early lift at a seat change — and inert keys sit outside the cap (indefinite doors are the DM's and unbounded by the engine) — PT §2.1's three clauses, so the person's and the party's `banKeys` keep ONE ban and ONE lift rule (EXECUTED as a model over master's one writer, `cure/D-CH-r4-probe/banKeysUndo.out`: after ten bans, one door indefinite, the keys are nine with eight windowed, the inert key kept and the oldest windowed evicted; a re-ban at one door leaves one key with the newer episode; a seat change at the indefinite door lifts nothing and keeps its key). The key form counts EVERY row of both tails, the DM's tick-less `CHANGE_RULING_POWER` included (PL §2: the tick form "cannot be placed by a tick"). A DM KILL/ASSIGN writes neither tail (PL accepts this), so the DM's KILL/ASSIGN card offers "lift this court's bans" as an option — the DM who unseats a ruler by hand may open the new court's pardon by hand, as PL's own KILL/ASSIGN card offers "ask the vassals".
- **A polity-wide ban** — the crown (a polity's root, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PL.md`) proclaims it through its BENCH (an emergent ban, or the DM's `banish` with scope `polity`): ONE edge at the root's own town's gate (truth, fails shut there) and, at each member the crown's reach covers, the `polity_ban` reason above (step 2). A member that ADMITS the banished paragon anyway at a `shaky` or firmer hold HARBOURS it, and the harbouring is PRICED, NEVER A BREAK (PL7: "these two acts are the ONLY triggers of a break"): CH REQUESTS the row through PL's door — `requestFealtyRow(worldState, snapshot, { relationshipKey: <the (root ↔ member) sworn edge>, kind: 'fealty_defied', episodeId: 'polity_ban:p:<wnpcId>:<banTick>' })` returns `{ fealtyRow, relationshipPatch }`, the closed row PL MINTS and PL7's deference patch (the price) — and the paragon pass writes ONE state-only relationship outcome carrying both through the one relationship writer, `relationshipEvolution.js :: applyRelationshipPatch(worldState, { relationshipKey, relationshipPatch, metadata: { fealtyRow }, proposalPayload: null }, now, edge)` (the landed precedent of a stage writing a state-only patch: `sovereigntyTransfer.js :: accrueSaleGrievance`; PL §5.5's STATE idiom); `rowId` keeps it exact-once, and the prefix `polity_ban:` is REGISTERED in PL's `FEALTY_EPISODE_PREFIXES` (OD-PL-46). A FREE corps admitted under the crown's ban (the corps arm's step 2) is harboured by the same row with the episode `polity_ban:u:<unitKey>:<banTick>`: PL's ONE ban grammar `polity_ban:<subjectKey>:<banTick>`, `subjectKey` RN's key — `p:<wnpcId>` for a person, `u:<unitKey>` for a company (PL §4.6, OD-PL-46; P40) — PS's `corps:` crossing prefix is a different occasion. WHO READS IT: the fealty question reads every row on the edge; the hold reads the defiance ONLY through its deference patch, inside `D̄` — its protection term `P` counts protection rows alone (PL §4.6); PL's break detector reads only new treaties and coalition joins and never the fealty record, so no harbouring row can open `fealty_break`. CH writes no patch and no row of its own. A real tension, never a veto. FLAGS: the `polity_ban` reason needs PL's `polityHoldEnabled` (the hold and `crownReachAt`); the harbouring row is a REQUESTED row, written under the requester's own flag ∧ `polityEnabled` (PL §8's REQUESTED family) — here `paragonGateEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled` — and it can fire only where the `polity_ban` reason does; `polityObligationsEnabled` no longer gates it; with the polity dark the admission is priced nowhere and the dossier notes the crown's word unheeded. No member carries a hard edge: a hard edge "FAILS SHUT" and would make harbouring impossible, against the owner's priced-input reading in the brief, so members carry the crown's ban as a town's-gate reason weighted by the hold, and only the crown's own town's gate carries the edge.
- **A region ban** (the DM's named set of settlements) is a hard edge on each listed settlement: the DM's word is canon.
- **The character's side.** A banned place is a barrier in the weekly choice (the candidate is removed); a defiant, brave or vengeful nature (§4.4) may instead choose to enter in secret along a hidden path (`HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN`) — a WAY it knows into that country (§5's trail arm on the ONE ladder — `knownTrailsOf`, the landed hidden price; with none known, the road itself, unseen only by the watch's draw) — and a court may find it: each week it stays, a keyed hash at the court's watch (ES's sweep when lit; a DRAFT base rate otherwise) ⇒ `banished_found` (`expelled · let_be`, default `expelled`; `expelled` reused in its landed meaning — the roads' detention outcome, a traveller turned out of a town; `overlooked` is a landed `PULL_BANDS` rung and never a verdict); there is no `seized` verdict — a paragon's custody waits on ES-2b's custody wave ("`banished_found` has no `seized`"). A ban lowers the paragon's regard of the court that set it (RN, reason `refused_at_gate`).
- **Belief on both sides.** A court bans on what it has heard; a paragon may not know of a new ban until word reaches where it stands (its picture); a court may fail to recognise a banished paragon arriving unannounced (the town's-gate ban-first rule reads the edge as truth only at the court's own town's gate, but whether the court NOTICES a secret entry is the watch's draw).
- **Actor refs.** The edge's key widens from a person id to an actor ref `{kind:'person'|'party', id?}` — W-H1's declared shape change, owned by PT for the party; paragons keep the person key unchanged (`{kind:'person', id: wnpcId}` resolves to the same map entry), and a corps keys `u:<unitKey>` (PT's shape 3; CH writes a company's edges).

**Tier.** `npcConsequencesEnabled` (the ledger and the edges) + `paragonGateEnabled`; the renown step `renownLeansEnabled`; the crown's ban `polityHoldEnabled`, its harbouring row `paragonGateEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled` (a REQUESTED row); a patrol's turn-back at the road `patrolsEnabled` (§5); a body's turn-back on a road or a trail THE BODIES' `bodiesEnabled` (the trail `hiddenWaysEnabled`); the attempt on a warden `bodiesEnabled`; the WATCH and the BENCH need persons lit (else the ruler); belief reads under a belief info mode; OMNISCIENT ARM: every court judges on the truth (fame and deeds known at once inside RN's projected scope). FIRST-VISIBLE: the town's-gate card the week it arrives ("Kell's Watch Captain shut Kell's gates to Sister Aude: a saint of the Ash in a town of the Lantern"). Counterforce: lapse, pardon, a seat change, harbouring, the paragon's own defiance.

---

## §12 FAILURE — BY DEGREES, NEVER DEATH (CH-8)

**12.1 The setting.** `failure ∈ fated · sure · fallible · frail` — ordered; it sets how often an EXERTION fails and how far a failure falls. Each exertion draws `hash01('paragon.fail.<wnpcId>.<tick>')` against `FAIL_CHANCE[failure]` (DRAFT: fated 0, sure low, fallible middling, frail high — UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster), raised by an active condition and by `dread`, lowered by a habit of exerting there.

**12.2 The ladder** (the depth drawn from `FALL_DEPTH[failure]`, DRAFT):
- `setback` — the effort comes to nothing this week (the lean does not apply). No deed.
- `stumble` — a deed of failure (RN): fame dips where it happened; regard of the town it failed falls a little.
- `disgrace` — as a stumble, and the employer's regard falls; when that fall would take the employer's regard to `mistrust` or end the retainer, a `paragon_disgraced` hearing (`disgraced · weathered`; default `disgraced`) — the DM may spare it.
- `capture` — NOT a rung of the engine's failure draw: the ladder tops out at `disgrace` ("until it lands the failure ladder stops at disgrace"); custody waits on ES-2b and the DM's own hand is the only hold (12.4).

A CORPS falls by its own ladder (§16b): `setback · stumble · desertion · sell-out`, never capture.

`fated` is the owner's earlier "always succeed": its exertions never fail by the engine's draw. It can still be refused, banned, out-dueled at a hearing, and decided against by the DM; "fated" never overrides a hearing.

**12.3 What failure never does.** It never kills (CH3); it never ends a story the DM is telling; a paragon is never removed from the ledger by the engine.

**12.4 CUSTODY — WAITS ON ES-2b's CUSTODY WAVE; UNTIL THEN, THE DM'S HAND.** ES-2b (custody) and WR-7d (ransom persistence) are OPEN by delegation (a VISIBLE row in ODQ §934.93's P5 addendum; the owner's "keep them dark" reverses it): the custody and ransom shapes written below ("*When ES-2b's custody wave lands*", and the ransom race for a paragon a body holds) are LIVE DESIGN, dispatching in their own waves — ES's custody wave FIRST, then THE BODIES' ransom race (BD-4) and CH's road arm. Everywhere this volume says a paragon's custody "waits on ES-2b", it means ES-2b's custody WAVE — an ORDER, no longer an owner gate. The champions brief's word, verbatim: "they can be taken ransomed depending on their role and situation and areas traveled". What does NOT change until that wave lands, and is not widened by the opening: the DM's hand (`authored.heldAt`) stays every world's only hold; the failure ladder still tops at `disgrace` (no capture rung is designed here — a rung would be new design); §11's road arm keeps `admitted · refused` (a body's TAKING of a paragon is THE BODIES' act on ES's hold, BD §9.10, never a new CH verdict); `banished_found` keeps `expelled · let_be`.

*What the code carries* (CONFIRMED at `81f1cd963`): `foreignGuestHold.js :: normalizeForeignGuestHold` refuses any row without an errand and an encounter (`if (!id || !npcId || !errandId || !encounterId || !captorId || !venueId …) return null;`), its one opener is `envoyInterceptionStage.js :: holdInterruptedEnvoy`, and ES-2 recorded why nobody else may open it: "The errand DTO refuses the held state for a row that carries no army-carried ENCOUNTER … Relaxing either shape is a persisted-schema question and this estate gates those on the owner" (`custodyWritten: false`). *The law:* a held paragon's custody WAITS ON ES-2b's custody wave (fold §3.3.13, OD-FC-60), and CH keeps NO landed custody arm as live design: no `jailed` (the H2 local verdict, `npcVerdictTable.js :: HOLDING_VERDICT`), no `paragon.held`, no `seized`, no second custody road. *Until ES-2b:* THE DM'S HAND is the only hold — the DM brings the paragon where it is held (`assign`) and writes `authored.heldAt` through the `introduce` revision; while it stands, the paragon neither moves nor exerts (§4.2), its page and its town's dossier say "held at <town>" in the DM's word, and it is released when the DM's revision clears the field. It is AUTHORED WHEREABOUTS, never an engine hold: no captor court, no ransom, no hearing (the DM's own act needs none), no custody receipt. The owner's §125.5 (a free paragon taken by bandits, released with no ransom) is the DM's to tell the same way.

*When ES-2b's custody wave lands* (LIVE DESIGN, above): a held paragon rides the generalized hold; the ransom is the registry's `ransom` card, the court's choices in its weeks `pay · hunt · wait` (the fold's `ransom` row, CLOCKED), the employer's answer read against RN's court regard of the paragon; the landed `ransomChoices.js :: HOME_ANSWERS` `pay · refuse · abandon` ("`Object.freeze(['pay', 'refuse', 'abandon'])`", CONFIRMED at `81f1cd963`) stay the court-to-court leaf's internal answers, never the card's words (its `refuse` and `abandon` only delay), and the leaf's `abandon`, where recorded, still marks the landed grievance `abandoned_to_captivity` (the fold §3.3.13) — CH its first consumer; the held villain's nature decides whether it talks (§9, road 4); the ransom's PERSISTENCE stays WR-7d's. A CORPS has no custody at all — it is defeated or disbanded (§16b).

*A paragon taken by a body* (THE BODIES' items 34 and 34b: "a NAMED person taken by a body (an envoy, a paragon; never the party …) is CUSTODY, which WAITS ON ES-2b"; the owner's ransom race, BOARD, the 18:54 stamp, verbatim: "this also changes the ransom rules if they are taken by a gang or conclave. if the gang can survive with teh NPC for long, then they will have the ransom granted, if they are defeated, then the captive goes free."): a BODY takes no paragon until ES-2b's wave lands (§11's road arm turns it back, nothing more). WHEN IT LANDS, a paragon held by a gang or a conclave is THE BODIES' ransom race on the registry's `ransom` row — the clock from capture scaled by RN's fame and office of the captive, the court's `pay · hunt · wait` in its week: PAY early ends the clock `ransom_granted`; a HUNT that defeats the body first is `captive_freed`; WAIT, and SURVIVING THE CLOCK is `ransom_granted` — the automatic resolution PAYS, the court's treasury into the body's sustenance, whether or not the court chose to: requirement 24(b) forces the hand that week and only the DM's own decision overrides it (requirement 24's `decide`, the fold's word rule, never "decree"). The outcomes are `ransom_granted · captive_freed · captive_released` (item 34b's closed words; the fold's custody rows), and the three closings are a PARTITION: `ransom_granted` the body SURVIVED the clock (the automatic resolution pays) or the court paid early; `captive_freed` the body was defeated first; `captive_released` ONLY the body's own act when its sustenance cannot hold the captive (it lets him go early, receipted) or the DM's decision — never the clock's automatic outcome. A FREE paragon's captors find no court to ransom it to (ODQ §125.5: "NO ransom is issued (no settlement tie to ransom against)"), so NO ransom clock runs — there is no treasury the automatic resolution could pay from — and the body holds the paragon until its sustenance cannot (it lets him go, `captive_released`, the body's own act, receipted), it is defeated (`captive_freed`) or the DM decides (vetoable: this reads the free paragon's case as no clock at all). A hunt that frees it mints the freer's deed (item 34b: "the freer's deed — the warden's, the paragon's") — the led hunt's ONE `hunt_won` (§2.2b); the engine never harms the captive (CH3). A conclave's taking of a captive follows its god's axes as a WEIGHT ("an evil creed readily; a good one rarely and in extremity — never 'never'"), THE BODIES' — never designed on before ES-2b's custody wave.

## §13 CONTESTS, NEMESES, TURNING POINTS, LINES AND ARCS (CH-8)

**The duel — `champion_duel`.** When a champion and a villain of ONE domain both exert in ONE settlement in one week AND their leans OPPOSE on the same target (the same creed, the same stressor, the same court's answer — a saint of a rival creed and a heretic both working against the town's patron are aligned and do not duel), the two leans are not simply multiplied: the week's contest is a CLIMAX hearing (`champion_prevails · villain_prevails · indecisive`) (`indecisive` is MN's word: `stand_off` is `armies_meet`'s option on the same docket surface, and `drawn` a landed literal). The default reads the two exertions (each one's fame band, its failure setting, a keyed hash `hash01('paragon.duel.<a>|<b>.<tick>')` over the codepoint-sorted pair); the winner's exertion is `exerted` and the loser's `voided`; `indecisive` lets both rows apply and cancel. A DECIDED duel mints ONE `duel_won` deed (§2.2b: the doer the winner, the target the loser); an `indecisive` duel mints none — no act kind carries it. If both stay and exert again, the next week mints a new duel (CLIMAX, not standing: each week is its own contest). `contest` is landed (the ladder contest, the patron contest) and is never used.

**The nemesis.** Three DECIDED duels between the same pair within a year (DRAFT) make a `nemesis` arc: both regard links fall toward `hatred` (RN reasons), and each one's proximity term toward the other strengthens (`pursue_nemesis`). The arc is a derived read of RN's PERSISTED deeds, never of the duel receipts (SP-9's records are "dropped and re-minted", L6): `paragonArcs.js` counts the `duel_won` deeds of source `paragon_act` between the pair in either direction — RN's `deedIndex(worldState).byDoer` at `p:<a>` with target `p:<b>`, and at `p:<b>` with target `p:<a>` — whose ticks fall within the last year; an `indecisive` week counts nothing (vetoable: the alternative registers an `indecisive` act kind and counts every duel) (biography is a query), with one Herald line when first recognised ("Captain Vey and the warlord Hask have met three times now; neither will let it rest").

**The turn — `polarity_turn` (proposal 4).** A champion whose `shame` has held at its top band for a season, whose employers' regard has fallen to `mistrust` or worse, and — once W-LIVES drift lights — whose `defining` axis has drifted toward its vice (`bandCrossingReceipt`), is PROPOSED to turn; a villain whose recent deeds' inherent value (RN) has been `heroic` or `admirable` for a season, with drift toward its virtue, is proposed to be redeemed. The engine proposes in the week the conditions are met; the hearing (`turns · holds`) defaults to `turns` only when all its conditions stand at their strongest band, otherwise `holds` — and on ANY advance longer than one week the default is `holds` regardless (a side never turns unseen across a long skip; the DM meets a turn at a week's advance). Polarity never flips silently (requirement 24). A turn writes the LIVING side `turned: { polarity, atTick }` through the ledger's writer — its one record (never a `marks.turnedAt`); `authored.polarity` — the DM's canon — is NEVER rewritten by the engine, every read of a paragon's side reads `turned` over `authored`, the DM's `introduce` revises the canon, and striking the turn's verdict clears `turned`; the arc words `downfall` (champion → villain) and `atonement` (villain → champion).

**The line — `never_line_tested` (proposals 1, 18).** When the weekly choice's best candidate would cross the paragon's `neverLine` (a rival creed's retainer, a covert retainer, leaving a town whose call in its domain stands `ruinous`, breaking an employer's retainer, exerting harm where the helpless stand for a villain whose line is `never_harm_the_helpless`) and that candidate's pull exceeds the line's resistance (the `defining` position's weight), the week mints a CLIMAX hearing (`holds_the_line · crosses`; default `holds_the_line` when the `defining` trait is the line's own virtue — a loyal paragon does not betray its employer — otherwise by the keyed hash). Crossing raises `shame`, deposits a drift lesson through the W-LIVES funnel, and is a deed (RN) — the `line_crossed` deed is its one record (never a `marks.crossedAt`).

**Named arcs (proposal 17).** Derived from receipts, never stored: `nemesis` (above), `downfall`, `atonement`, `long_road` (in its calling, it has stood in six settlements within a year — a pilgrimage, a circuit, a campaign; DRAFT). Each surfaces once in the Herald when first recognised and lives on the paragon page.

**Tier.** Duels and lines: the person tier + `paragonGiftsEnabled`; turns: the person tier (the drift arm when `characterDriftEnabled` lights). FIRST-VISIBLE: the hearing card of its week.

## §14 THE CALL BOARD — ONE SEAM EVERY CHARACTER WEIGHS (CH-2; proposal 11)

`src/domain/paragon/callBoard.js :: callBoard(worldState, snapshot, observerId)` — a PURE read, memoized per snapshot (the `tickIndices.js` WeakMap idiom) and per observer within a pass (the `applyForeignPrimacy` Map idiom), never a store ("Beliefs are derived ON DEMAND and NEVER stored per pair"). It returns, per domain, the CALLS — places that need that calling (`side: 'help'`) or where a villain can do the most harm (`side: 'harm'`) — each `{ settlementId, domain, side, band, source }` with `band` in the landed SEVERITY words (`bandFamilies.js :: SEVERITY_LADDER` = `glancing · telling · grave · ruinous`, reused in their landed meaning: how severe the need is).

| Domain | Sources (each an existing read) |
|---|---|
| faith | the faith contest's conversion ripeness for a creed (`deityLocalStrength`'s inputs as the observer believes them: hardship, prosperity, the seated patron's weakness, a foothold of believers, persecution risk); a creed's `heresyStain` |
| health | the `disease_outbreak` stressor's severity; the town's `care01`; no healer paragon present |
| war | live sieges (`display/warStatus.js :: liveSieges`), war fronts, the `wartime` and `siege` stressors |
| diplomacy | war-weary courts (war exhaustion), open pact proposals, live wars between courts |
| information | the `infiltration` stressor; courts whose espionage doctrine demands (when ES lights) |
| wildland | `monster_raider_pressure` (ambient, not menace-held); menaces pressing (MN's record) where MN's warden terms are lit (§6.2); a DEN — an untied-held site attached to a settlement (a gang's or a beast's, THE BODIES' holder ref — never a cult or a conclave: a conclave holds as ST's `faith` holder) (ST's `sitesNear` lists the CANDIDATE sites; a den is a call to an observer ONLY where ST's ONE belief read holds it, `believedDenOf(worldState, <the observer court>, siteId, snapshot).known` — for a paragon the court of the town it stands in, its picture being that town's belief (CH7) — and NEVER BY PRESENCE: a `keptSecret` den is known only by a WITNESS (a patrol's look, the warden, the holder's own act seen on that court's land — written into the holder's one belief by its owner's door) and an unheard one not at all; ST's read names its own omniscient arm — a kept-secret site known to no court by the truth arm, "the witness wins" — and CH never re-reads it; `side: 'help'`), where ST is lit; where THE PATROLS are lit, a `patrol_report` of a den or a band that has ARRIVED (road (b) below) (PR item 14(c)) |
| trade | `market_shock`; EC's market condition bands when EC lights |
| order | `rebellion`, `insurgency`, `political_fracture`; legitimacy below the bands the gates read |
| order — `captive_held` | THE OPPORTUNITY's source row (OP §9): `{ settlementId: <the captive's HOME>, domain: 'order', side: 'help', band, source: { kind: 'captive_held', venueId, captorId, toldBy: { road: 'roads_ransom' \| 'ransom_demand' \| 'telling', ref, tick } } }`, MINTED ONLY FROM THE ARRIVED RECORD that carried the hold home — a roads ransom its court pays (`toldBy.tick` = `RansomRec.startedTick`), WR-7d's persisted ransom DEMAND arrived (its `demandLeg`), or a capture TELLING arrived by RN's `heardAt` (once RN's P7 row accepts one) — its `venueId` and `captorId` COPIED FROM THAT RECORD, never from `foreignGuestHoldsOf` (the truth hold), in EVERY info mode (the omniscient arm knows a row that exists, never mints one); CH's row cites the arrival record as its source; its `harm` twin at the VENUE town, MINTED FROM THE JAILER's own hold record (a court knows its own cells — road (a) below is lawful for the holder; `jailerOf`, the occupier in an occupied town), KNOWN by presence in the belief presets and on the omniscient presets' board as the truth BY THE PRESET'S LAW (stated, not a leak); both banded in the SEVERITY words by the captive's standing; BOTH EXCLUDED FROM PT's read (no `party_request` source) |
| the hunting callings — `wildland` · `war` · `order` | a BOUNTY a court has POSTED on a NAMED body (THE BODIES' item 33: "it may POST A BOUNTY on a body … which enters the landed CALL BOARD … where PARAGONS, CORPS and the PARTY may answer"; item 32b: only a named body), `side: 'help'`, banded by the body's band as the observer believes it (THE BODIES' belief record of the body, item 45) and by the bounty's terms; ONE CALL PER BOUNTY, ONE DOMAIN — the call's own domain governs (BD §9.9: "`order` for a gang or a flock, `wildland` for a beast"): a gang's bounty is an `order` call, an `outlawed` conclave's an `order` call (BD §7.1's quarry rule — a `curbed` or `permitted` conclave carries none; never a `faith` call: the followers act, the god does not), a beast pack's a `wildland` call; a paragon of ANY hunting calling (`wildland` · `war` · `order`) reads a posted bounty it knows of as a call in ITS calling — the hunt is its trade (§4.2 `hunt_body`) — so a warden weighs a gang's `order` bounty, while every other reader (PT's request, the board's ranking, the party) reads the call's one domain; its SOURCE is `{ kind: 'body_bounty', courtId, bodyId }` — THE BODIES' record (`courtStances[courtId][<the body's subject key>].bounty`, BD §2.2: "`bounty: { band, sinceTick }`"), which carries no id of its own, named by the posting court and the body, so PT's request takes `need: { bodyId }` from `source.bodyId`; and a bounty ANSWERED stays on the board until its hunt closes (the counterforce row below) |

BELIEF (`beliefMap.js`'s `BeliefRecord` carries no plague severity, unrest, market shock, siege, menace, war weariness or faith ripeness): a call is KNOWN to an observer by one of three roads, never by truth outside the omniscient arm — (a) PRESENCE: the observer stands at the call's settlement (a paragon sees the town it stands in; a court knows itself) — NEVER for a DEN, whose holder is a belief and is known only through ST's `believedDenOf` (the wildland row above; the veil); (b) ARRIVAL: a news telling of the call's source (the stressor's birth or escalation, the siege, the war) has ARRIVED at the observer's settlement on the rumour road — RN's `fameIn` arrival test applied to the source's telling; (c) A BELIEF BAND the observer's `beliefRecord` already carries for the subject (`conditionsBands`, `scarcityBands`, `devotionBand`, `observanceLabel`, `faithLabel`, `strengthBand`, `readiness`). Sources with NO belief arm — faith ripeness beyond those bands, legitimacy, a market's condition until EC — are known by presence alone. Under the omniscient info mode (the default and three other presets) the board is the truth. A paragon's `picture` (4.7) carries what it learned where it stood. The PARTY volume reads the same board for the requests actors bring to the party (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PT.md`), so one seam serves every character and no system wires a special case per archetype. Counterforce: a call stands until its SOURCE resolves (the stressor leaves `active`, the siege ends); a call answered by a paragon of its domain present ranks LOWER for the next comer and is never CLOSED by the answer — a doctor easing a plague leaves the plague standing, and PT's request reads the same rule. Each call names its source by the stressor's (or the siege's, the war's) id; a menace's driven record must keep its id on release — CH depends on MN-2's answer (keep the id and change only the policy fields, or a `supersededBy` link CH follows), pinned with a call across a lift. Menace-held pressure is a warden's call where MN's declared warden terms are lit (MN §5.2, §5.5 — §6.2); where they are dark it is not, so no warden is sent where its exertion would do nothing.
PT's `party_request` NEVER reads a `captive_held` call, help or harm: the help call's settlement is the captive's home, so no jailer can ask through it to have his own cells broken, and the harm twin is a villain's chance, never a request (OP §9).
